A PRODUCTION mutation now carries four admission conditions, each evidenced: admitted as the intended single semantic atom with the diff being that atom and nothing else; the module still elaborates so the failure is semantic; the observed failure is FOR THE CLAIMED PROPERTY rather than a parse error, unrelated broken elaboration, or a setup or harness failure; and the failing subject is named with command and output. A mutant that fails to compile has demonstrated nothing about the theorem.

Revision 2 required that "the module still elaborates" and stated that "a mutant that fails to compile has demonstrated nothing about the theorem". That BANS THE MAIN LEAN PROOF FALSIFICATION METHOD: mutating a production definition normally makes the dependent theorem's PROOF stop elaborating, and that failure IS the kill. I had written an admission condition that would have excluded exactly the evidence a Lean mutation campaign produces. Corrected: the well-typedness requirement is on the MUTATED PRODUCTION DEFINITION, never on the theorem meant to fail; a named theorem proof failing, a #guard failing, or a semantic check failing BECAUSE the definition no longer has the claimed property is all relevant RED evidence; only syntax errors, import errors, setup or harness failures and elaboration failures unrelated to the claimed property are excluded; and an intended failed theorem proof must not be reclassified as infrastructure noise.
2026-09-05T11:04:31+01:00 CORRECT-REV3-2 REQUIRED-INPUT is NOT the Cartesian product D1a x D1b. Corrected to derive the two sets plus the ACTUAL SEMANTIC OWNERSHIP RELATION between them — which theorem properties a given guard or effect atom genuinely constrains. The obligation is that every required guard/effect atom and every in-scope theorem property has RELEVANT coverage, not that every pairing exists. A single admitted atom may constrain and close several theorem rows provided each row's property-specific failure is actually observed. Manufacturing unrelated mutants to fill a cross product is forbidden: an atom paired with a theorem it does not constrain produces a meaningless row and inflates the denominator, which is the same failure mode as the withdrawn 6/224.
2026-09-05T11:04:31+01:00 CORRECT-REV3-3 costing must preserve isolation and name its measurement kind. Separately admitted single-atom variants MAY share compilation infrastructure or be scheduled together, but mutating several atoms in one subject is ONE MUTANT WITH AN AMBIGUOUS CAUSE and cannot count as an independent kill of each, however many rows it appears to touch — a costing shortcut that breaks isolation is not a saving but a false result. Each measurement must state whether it is a full cold build, an incremental production rebuild, a proof or check elaboration, or a runtime replay; those differ by orders of magnitude, are reported separately and are never averaged together. Also gave the always-true checker case its conditional limit: a surrounding test CAN AND SHOULD detect that mutant, and if it survives the required surrounding check that survival is ITSELF evidence of an assurance gap — while still never showing the altered checker can reject.
2026-09-05T11:04:31+01:00 CORRECT-REV3-4 the entry-point table is NOT an allowlist and must not seed one. Verified the desk's point directly: my revision-2 table omitted KelGroups.Integration ENTIRELY and most of KelGroups.Validate. Added on inspection — Validate carries validateProposal, validateApproval, validateDirectAdmission, validateBaseMutation, validateBaseApproval, validateBase and validateEvent; Integration carries applyIntegratedEvent, foldIntegrated, tryEnactBase, commitBaseChange, admitMemberInto, enactMutation and the BaseHook / IntegratedAppFold reached hooks. The omission is itself the evidence that a hand-written entry-point list goes stale the moment it is written, so revision 3 requires S3 to RE-DERIVE entry points and reached hooks at its own base and to treat any disagreement with the table as a finding ABOUT THE TABLE.

2026-09-05T12:30:05+01:00 S4-REV2 the corrections were mine and several were category errors. v1 collapsed DECIDABILITY and CORRESPONDENCE onto one axis: possessing Decidable p does NOT prove correspondence with an independently implemented Bool consumer, and definitional equality and a proved equivalence are DIFFERENT EVIDENCE. Split into DEFINITIONAL-IDENTITY (closes by reduction, cite the reduction), PROVED-EQUIVALENCE (a theorem relating the Prop to an independently implemented Bool), DECISION-AVAILABLE (an instance exists so the Prop is executable — explicitly NOT correspondence and may not be reported as if it were), MISSING-CORRESPONDENCE split into mirror-exists where ONLY THE THEOREM IS OWED and no second mirror is ever required versus no-mirror where both are, NOT-EXECUTABLE with bounded evidence stated as bounded, and NOT-ESTABLISHED. UNDECIDABLE is now a claim requiring a proved reduction or cited authority, never a default: this concrete Reach is an inductive predicate over this machine and is NOT undecidable by its name or by generic reachability folklore, and failure to synthesize an instance proves nothing. Added explicitly: do not force a conclusion to fit a box — the taxonomy serves the finding, not the reverse.

2026-09-06T04:56:12Z  S3-PARSER-ROOT-CAUSE-AND-A-SECOND-DEFECT-THE-DESK-DID-NOT-NAME  Diagnosed the hang precisely from the retained argv rather than relaying the description. The loop is while(j<L.length && !DECL_RE.test(L[j]) || /^\s/.test(L[j]) && false){} with an EMPTY BODY and with neither j nor L modified, so once the condition holds it can never terminate - which is the defect the desk identified. FOUND A SECOND DEFECT INSIDE THE SAME CONDITION that the desk did not name: by JavaScript precedence it parses as (j<L.length && !test(L[j])) || (test(L[j]) && false), and the right disjunct is ALWAYS FALSE, so the author's intended continuation clause is dead code and the guard silently reduces to the first conjunct. Fixing only the empty body would leave a guard that does not mean what it reads. Told the owner to fix both. Bound the requirements for any replacement mechanical parser: BOUNDED TERMINATION with a hard iteration or line ceiling that aborts loudly rather than spinning, and EXPLICIT treatment of unsupported syntax that names what it could not parse instead of skipping silently - plus the standing limit that regex and text search are a review aid and never a complete Lean declaration or call-graph oracle, so the required semantic assessment must not be weakened into a token-hit inventory. Also required it to journal the instrument failure itself with its argv and real duration. Re-sent both corrections that had been queued behind the hang - the future-dated 05:45Z START preserved as history with an appended live-UTC correction, and the wrong PATH-based zero-execution reasoning restated as the explicit mandate it is - supplying the current host UTC and the canonical status-event helper path so it need not guess a stamp.
